Review Text Im going to offer a dissenting opinion here and say that Grand Theft Auto or at least this version of it for the Xbox is not quite as wonderful as some of its fans would have you believe I picked up the Double Pack after hearing friends who had PS2s rave about these games for years I also read a few reviews that said even though the graphics may be a little out of date the gameplay remained very compelling Well GTA may have been groundbreaking in its day but I was less than impressed after making several attemps to wade into these two games The graphics even though they may have been updated a little more for the Xbox are bad Distractingly bad The cars look okay but the buildings in the distance when you can see them remind me of the background of a Sega Genesis game I am not kidding Youll see the same three or four characters repeated over and over again on the street saying the same things The way the people move is also very cartoonish and offputting The control scheme is frustratingly awkward as well Whoever translated them over from the PS2 controller to the Xboxs did a horrible job The combat system is poorly designed and the way in which you target and fire guns leaves much to be desired Finally within my first fifteen or twenty minutes of playing the game I noticed two glaring bugs the first of which was a loss of sound and the second a slowing down of the framerate to a glacial pace Both of these occurred in Vice City for what its worth If youre an Xbox owner and have not played GTA before I would recommend just renting these games to see if theyre for you instead of just buying them on the basis of word of mouth The dialogue in the game is pretty funny and driving around the city is fun but the missions are fairly repetitious and get to be more of a chore than anything else Ultimately I found the booklets that came with each game more entertaining than the game itself

Review Text Rockstars GBA conversion of the first Max Payne game is surprisingly faithful to the original I had originally thought that some drastic changes for the game would be in store in bringing it to the GBA but the only major change is shifting the game from a third person perspective to an isometric point of view The only other real change to the game is the opening sequence when you play as Max and find your wife and baby dead is completely gone as are the dream sequences that follow The same comic style cut scenes are still here though and the character designs and animations resemble their larger console counterparts surprisingly well The controls are tight for the most part as well but this GBA conversion of Max Payne isnt without its flaws Thanks to the isometric point of view it is too easy to enter a room and get shot first by enemies that arent even on the screen yet Also the look of the game is kind of dark but if played on a GameBoy Advance SP the lighting can be improved Those few flaws aside though Max Payne on the GBA is a surprisingly good game that manages to be almost as satisfying as its larger console bretheren

tactics with your soontobe companions at the character selection screen In a similar vein to an RPG each of the eight playable characters that you can select brings completely different abilities to the table that you must factor in Do you bring a lockpicker Someone with extra inventory spots Or rely on firepower with a weapons expertUnlike the other games in the series zombies can pass from room to room and are often found in packs Taking this newfound hunting prowess into account Capcom has implemented a handful of new techniques that players can use to survive You can slide under beds hide in lockers and even repel a zombie attack by forcing their head away from your neck with enough breathing room to raise your pistol and shoot them directly between the eyes Youll even find strategy in the reloading of weapons For instance a box of shells takes more time to load than a clip As annoying as it is to watch your character get munched because he couldnt load the rounds quick enough I really like this aspect It makes you keep an eye on every little detailWhere Outbreak excels in engrossing multiplayer and creative gameplay the overall design of the game is very odd Rather than creating one lengthy quest that players can really sink their teeth into Capcom opted to break up the game into five separate scenarios that are roughly 45 minutes each If you die youll turn into a Review Text The idea of an online Resident Evil game has been floating around for years Well Capcom finally delivered theyre much hyped and promised online RE game with Resident Evil Outbreak Taking place in the zombie infested Raccoon City you control one of eight ordinary people each has his or her own skill that can be put to good use in various situations as you run and gun your way to escape the city The gameplay elements are basically identical to those of the previous game in the long running series although there are some differences The control scheme has been changed for the better meaning you can now run in different directions without having to hold down a shoulder button However Outbreak has plenty of its own frustrations When playing offline your AI controled allies perform acts which range from helping keeping you alive to doing some nonsensical and almost stupid acts When playing online you are joined by three other players and the game is seperated by five episodic chapters Where the game is really hurt is where the series has always had its strengths the storyline is almost nonexistant and the characters arent much to care for There are still loads of CG movies and unlockable costumes and such but nothing else much in terms of replay value besides the online capability The graphics are solid and the score manages to give the game a creepy atmosphere but one cant help but wonder what a marvel of a game this could have been Outbreak is still more than worth a look for fans of the series and survival horror in general though and the online capability makes this definitely worth a look

Review Text Yu Yu Hakusho Dark Tournament is based on an anime series Yusuke Urameshi the main character is a 14 year old He was killed and brought back to life and now is able to see ghostsThe game puts Yusuke into an arena tournament fighting situation with the familiar friends and foes from the show There is a training mode to help you get started and skirmish mode to practice your moves Then there are arcade and dark tournament modes to get you into actual gameplay moving through levels of difficultyYou cant really compare the graphics in this game to other fighting games like Mortal Kombat The point of Dark Tournament is to feel like youre immersed in the Yu Yu Hakusho world and it does this quite nicely The anime style and backgrounds are all very much of the Yu Yu Hakusho style There are several different arenas to fight in and many characters to choose from Each character has his or her own style and lookIn addition to the basic fighting game there is also a built in token game You earn tokens when you fight You then move to the token game to use them The token game is very much like a strategy card game using a hexagonal based board This addition makes the overall game much more long term You have incentive to keep fighting to earn tokens Then in the token game you can build up your token stash to do better and betterDefinitely a game that any Yu Yu Hakusho fan will love and play for a long time

across the world with no apparent cause Meanwhile patent lawyer George Stobbart is flying to the Congo to meet with a Dr Cholmondeley to discuss a new source of renewable energy when his plane crashes stranding him in the Jungle At the same time in Paris journalist Nico Collard is framed for the murder of a computer analyst on the cusp of uncovering an international conspiracy Its once again up to George and Nico to solve the mystery and save the planet from an ancient evilUnlike previous games in the series you control the characters along a 3D plane and interact with the environment While its an unfortunate departure from the lovingly painted backdrops of the first two games it the environments are still well designed and the controls are quite smooth In terms of graphics they are alright writing The series trademark humor and good writing return This along with a really great and atmospheric soundtrack make you interested in seeing moreThe game is good Unfortunately it is to me the far into the series then I recommend this game Despite its shortcomings Its still a good Broken Sword game and a pretty solid adventure game to boot

Review Text One of the biggest trends in video games right now its revamping old games for a new generation Metroid Zero Mission with other games like Tomb Raider Anniversary and to some extend New Super Mario Bros all claim to not be a remake but a update of the original concept MetroidL Zero Mission retells as the box art puts it the real story of Samus Arans very first adventure Taking its cue from the mid 80s NES classic Metroid Zero Mission is a throughly enjoyable and classic 2D gaming experience for the GBA or Nintendo DSAs the adventure begins anyone familiar with the original game will notice that the enviroments look similar to the original with different layouts and gaming textures New areas weapons and enemies have all been added to create the redux of a classic and oft released video gameThese updates for the most part succeed The graphics are crisp clear and detailed Especially when playing on the high res DS The control is what you expect from Nintendoright on and intuitive The music is also a well produced effort with many familiar tracks being remixed for this versionBut what is missing is the sense of adventure and scifi from the orginial Maybe its because the series is so well known and Samus true identity is readily accessible by many fans Also many of the updates for example the new grip ability take away some of the challenge that made completing all those old games so rewardingIn the end Zero Mission is a great solid and engaging game Its fun to see a different side of the Metroid mythos and the added graphical powers and newly introduced areas of Zebes hauntingly familiar to those fans of Super Metroid help add depth the story A definate treat for those fans of old school sidescrolling actionadventure gaming

Review Text Its a long story but I got one of these memory cards used It came along as a package deal with the system and a couple controllers I used it nearly constantly in my free time for about six months before I got my PS2 Even though it was badly beaten up by its previous owner the card has never had a single issue connecting to the GameCube reading or writing data It has plenty of space for the dozen games I own with more left over if I get any new ones My friend has had his for years and he says it still works like the day he first got itJust a heads up there isnt a battery backup or anything to run out but because of the way flash memory operates it will theoretically only retain any given save file for around a decade Anytime you write to that save again it would restart the clock and give you another ten years It has to do with how long the memory physically retains the imprint of the save The same principle applies to common USB drives or camera memory cards It really wont affect you unless youre planning to keep the machine a really really long time but its good to be aware

Review Text Its been quite the ride From the original arcade series to console Deltas to little known Leos RType has a rich history RType Final enters the fight with high expectationsFrom a glance this is the RType we know and love We get side scrolling levels powerups that absorb bullets and Bydo enemies that are easily recognizable as Bydo RType Final has good consistency with the rest of the seriesWhere this game really shines is customization Alternate ships powerups and colors await you These unlock as you play more and offer good incentive to experiment If you want to unlock everything youll be here a long timeThe actual gameplay and level design Im afraid to admit has some bad pacing issues The first level says it all Fly around a bit Oh a few enemies Shoot them Now fly around for a while before finding more Yawn Now check out the Flying Battleship stage a RType staple Here the level moves at an astonishingly sluggish pace with so much slowdown it makes SNES Super RType look speedySure RType is known for being a slower paced shmup believe me Ive played them all extensively however its never been this slow Boredom sets in waiting for the more interesting parts to kick inLuckily there is a decent amount of challenge to be found and some sections will likely take a lot of tries to perfect The difficulty settings are functional and impact the difficulty well for those who want to use themSo I guess it depends on what youre looking for If you want a shmup with lots of options and replay value theres a lot to do here If youre looking for a solid gameplay experience other RType games offer vastly more RType Final is an aptly named title in more ways than one because its the final RType game Id suggest playing
